[x] medium: bug hunt round 9 W1-1: 'array_copy', 'arrays_equal', 'read',
    'write' and the sector functions compute the count (and the start)
    before the arrays or the buffer; 'assert_same_in_either_order' sees
    only writes to variables, so output, 'exit' and panics on both sides
    run in the reverse of the source order ('array_copy(a[p(1)], b, q(2))'
    with 'p' and 'q' printing gives 'Q P'; 'array_copy(a[5], b, x / 0)'
    panics 'division' before 'bounds')
    => plan: every operand in source order: the addresses, then the count,
       then the range check of each start
       1. the address walk checks a run-time start as 0 <= start <= length
          ('count_alone', the end allowed) and copies it into a register the
          caller allocated before the walk ('lea_request::start_register',
          replaces 'reg_count'); a constant start or an unindexed array
          needs no register
       2. after the count: 'range(start, count, length)' for a run-time
          start, 'count_alone(count, length - start)' for a constant one;
          the start registers are freed there, in stack order
       3. 'machine::address_emitter' loses its count; both backends emit the
          addresses, then 'emit_count', which compiles the count and the
          range checks; 'bulk_operands::run_time_emitters' holds the starts
          between the two
       4. io: descriptor, buffer address, count, start, range checks
       5. removed: 'assert_same_in_either_order', its two callers and
          messages, README 7.14
    => rejected: output, 'exit' and checked operations as effects of the
       order check (a new concept, rejects 'array_copy(a[i], b, n - 1)'
       under 'overflow'); a range check by addresses (the end of an inner
       array of 'm[i].v[j]' needs 'i')
    => invariants: the operands of these built-ins are emitted in source
       order; no element is read or written before every range check
       passed; a start register is held from its address to its check
    => tests: W1's reproducers (print order, 'exit' and 'division' against
       'bounds'), range edges (end at the length, start at the length with
       count 0, negative start or count, 'start + count' past the maximum),

    => done (2026-10-27): the plan as written; 'assert_not_reached_by_call'

    M3.1. a noinline call computes the index of a place argument after the

       ('held_registers::release' before 'call_function'); tests 1729-1731;

    M3.1 plan (2026-10-27, go-ahead given)
       problem: 'noinline_call::compile_arguments_and_call' compiles every
       temporary argument first, then 'populate_frame' computes the address
       of each place argument ('slot_address_of', 'get_lea_operand'); a call
       in the index of a place, 'v[w()]', runs after the later arguments;
       the checks assume source order ('assert_not_written_later' note: "a
       call among the earlier operands has run before the later ones",
       README 7.14 "every node emits its operands in the order of the
       source"), so the emitted code is not the checked code (README 3.1)
       design: one pass in argument order; a temporary argument is compiled
       into its temporary, a place argument gets its address at its
       position, held in a register when it has a run-time index, until
       the frame is written; 'noinline_arg' keeps the address operand
       ('slot_address_of' returns it, no path walk at the frame);
       'held_registers' moves from 'populate_frame' to
       'compile_arguments_and_call', freed before 'call_function' as now
       (an address in a register is loaded after the registers are saved);
       the result slot and the frame and stack checks stay in
       'populate_frame'
       rejected:
         1. the address written to its frame slot at its position: a
            noinline call in a later argument puts its frame at the same
            'next_frame_address' and overwrites the slot
         2. reject an index call that conflicts with a later argument (the
            M3.2 rule): accepts less, also for the inlined call to keep
            the verdict independent of inlining; the emitted code stays
            unlike the checked code
         3. the index of a place compiled into an integer temporary: a
            second form of a path (the index replaced), several indexes per
            path, for one case
         4. hold only places with a call in an index: two orders of
            argument code, the exception the design removes
       invariants: the code of each argument runs in argument order, also
       for a noinline call (the note of 'assert_not_written_later' and
       README 7.14 hold without exception); a place's address register is
       held from its argument to the write of the frame
       cost: a place with a run-time index before a temporary argument holds
       its register across the temporary's code (needed by the order, the
       scratch rule allows it); more x86_64 "cannot allocate register"
       rejections are possible (the open many-indexed-arguments item);
       measure listing growth and the verdicts of every test and of the
       fuzzers
       tests: 'pick(v[w()], g + 1)' (214) and 'pick(v[h()], f())' (10)
       noinline on both targets, equal to the inlined results; a place with
       a run-time index before a temporary that has a noinline call (the
       held register survives the nested call); listings and verdicts of
       every other test compared with HEAD
    => done (2026-10-27): M2.1, M2.2, M3.1, M3.2 fixed, M3.3 and the M1
       index note documented in README 8; the phase checks clean but for
       new instances of the decided register-conflict class ('gen-reject',
       also at HEAD)

[x] hard: design review round 18 (2026-10-25, judged as a whole from six
    angles: architect A, extensibility X, measured outliers M, consistency C,
    invariants in types I, documentation D; findings in the session
    scratchpad r18; verdicts: good (A, X, M, I), good not yet great (C, D);
    spot-checked: X2 (the default 'visit_call_effects' does nothing), A.1,
    M1, D6): what stands between "good" and "great" is how the core
    contracts are carried: the operand order of a node is written three
    times and its no-UB walks default to "nothing" (X2, M1); the built-ins

       => F.8 plan (2026-10-25, recommended: plan first, it is a big
          change; waits for go-ahead):
          problem: an operator is read character by character four ways
          ('expr_arith::peek_operator' and 'consume_peeked_operator',
          'is_comparison_next' on a probe, 'expr_bool::
          parse_comparison_operator' with 'put_back_char', 'unary_ops' with
          'put_back_char' per character); its whitespace is kept in
          separate tokens ('ws_before_ops_', 'ws_pre_op'/'ws_post_op',
          'ws_pre_'/'ws_between_'), so the rule of README 7.2 (a node keeps
          every token) does not hold for operators
          design: 'tokenizer::next_operator() -> std::optional<token>' reads
          the longest operator at the cursor with the whitespace before and
          after, like 'is_next_char_token' (table: '<<' '>>' '<=' '>=' '=='
          '!=' before the one-character ones); 'peek_operator_text()' for
          the lookahead sites on a copy; each enum gets a text table beside
          it ('arithmetic_operator', 'comparison_operator', the unary ops)
          and a parser keeps the operator token and maps its text; a list
          that ends puts the whole operator token back
          ('put_back_token'), which already exists; 'source_to' writes the
          kept tokens; the whitespace-only tokens, 'consume_peeked_operator',
          'put_back_char', 'is_peek_char2' go, the character methods that
          other sites still need stay
          rejected: keep the characters and only share one reader per
          parser, the whitespace tokens and the four techniques stay; a
          full operator lexer pass before parsing, the parser decides by
          context ('<' of a generic call, '-' unary or binary)
          invariants: an operator token's text is one of its enum's table;
          'next_operator' never splits '<<' or '<='; the parse consumes the

          tests: '--reproduce-source' equal on every test and the
          fuzz-exprs spelling mode; listings and error output byte-equal on
          both suites; the reject and compiler fuzzers; a lone '=' or '!'
          keeps its message and location (a test each if none exists)
          steps: 1 tokenizer reader and tables (no user); 2 'unary_ops';
          3 'expr_bool'; 4 'expr_arith' with 'is_comparison_next' and

    expressions, statements and calls, tables and checks, backends, a

    A. fixes first (failing test first, both targets, inline and noinline)
       A.1. the checked order is not the emitted order for 'array_copy' and
            'arrays_equal': both backends compile the count before the
            addresses, the check assumes list order; 'array_copy(a[g()], b,
            n)' with 'g' writing 'n' is accepted and copies with the old 'n'
            (confirmed on x86_64, exits 0, left to right gives 3)
            => design: the bulk machine operations call the emitters in
               source order (first, second, count); the count register is
               reserved before the addresses, filled last; 'list_operand'
               states "the list is in the order the code computes it"
            => rejected: compiling every operand with a call into a register
               first (costs registers, hides the order again)
       A.2. rv32i 'call_function' lowers a far frame address without keeping
            the slot register 's11', x86_64 keeps it ('slot_kept'); a test
            that holds the scratch registers before 's11' decides whether it
            is reachable; the fix is H.3
       => A done (2026-10-24): A.1 redecided (user): the count stays first
          (the range checks of the starts need it, 'read'/'write' alike); a
          list is accepted only when the emitted order and the source order
          give the same result, 'assert_not_written_after' beside
          'assert_not_written_later', used by 'bulk_operands' and
          'stmt_builtin_io' (tests 1650-1653); A.2 with H.3:
          'machine::call_function' is the one call sequence with the slot
          register kept, the backends give 'save_call_registers',
          'restore_call_registers', 'emit_call', 'is_stack_pointer'; no
          listing changed

[x] hard: review round 12 and the refactors that remove its bug classes
    (step 4, the plan); X (codegen, 41k programs) and B (sanitizer fuzz, 1.4M
    runs) clean; bug history: r12 scratchpad bug-history.md
    1. design: no undefined behavior through what a called body does
       1.1. problem: the checks see what a statement names, not what a call
            in it reads or writes; each check decides a call's effects
            itself or not at all
            1.1.1. C-1: argument order is not defined, 'f(arr[i], h(i))'
                   with 'h' incrementing 'i' exits 10 inline and 20
                   noinline; a bare name and an expression differ; the
                   built-ins compile the count before the indexes
            1.1.2. C-2: 'gx = k + gw()' with 'gw' writing 'gx' exits 11 on
                   x86_64 and 2 on rv32i ('destination_is_clobbered_before_
                   read' sees only the statement's reads)
            1.1.3. C-3: 'i == h(i)' reads 'i' after 'h' wrote it, 'i + 0 ==
                   h(i)' before ('resolve_cmp')
            1.1.4. the open item "a call in a value written in parts reads,
                   in its body, a part written before it" (below)
            1.1.5. V-3, conservative: 'init(res)', 'res.set(v)' and 'foo
                   res.a' count as a read of the whole place, "use of
                   uninitialized variable 'res'"
       1.2. decided (user, 2026-10-23): evaluation is left to right; an
            argument or operand that a later call in the same statement may
            write is rejected, e.g. "argument 1 'i' may be written by 'h' in
            argument 2, use a temporary variable"
       1.3. design: 'body_uses' already records per body the parameters and
            globals it reads and writes, callees included, but only the
            shared-storage check of a call reads it; it becomes the one
            effect summary every check reads:
            1.3.1. the evaluation order check (1.2) for call arguments,
                   operands of arithmetic and comparisons, and built-in
                   operands, one function used by all of them
            1.3.2. rule A and 'destination_is_clobbered_before_read' add
                   the reads and writes of a call's body (C-2, 1.1.4)
            1.3.3. definite assignment: a binding use gets "written whole
                   before any read", so 'init(res)' assigns (V-3); needs a
                   fixed point for noinline recursion
            => rejected: an overlap check per site (C-1, C-2, C-3 each),
               the same decision made three times
            => rejected: loading a later-written operand into a register
               first, it hides the order instead of defining it
            => invariant: an accepted statement gives one result whether
               its calls are inline or noinline, on every target
            => plan the steps (mechanical move of 'body_uses' queries
               first, then each check) before code, wait for go-ahead
    2. undefined behavior catalog in 'etc/docs/README.md': one row per
       class (aliasing, uninitialized read, evaluation order, bounds,
       overflow, division, stack depth of noinline recursion, what each
       opt-out option allows) with how it is handled (rejected or checked
       at run time) and the tests that cover it; a review round checks the
       rows
    3. runtime oracle: random programs with side-effecting calls, each
       compiled inline and noinline, on x86_64 and rv32i; every accepted
       program must give one result (extends the brute force of the
       aliasing check, README 3); a script under 'qa/coverage', ask first
    4. refactors (user, 2026-10-23: stop the review rounds; remove whole
       classes of bugs, the language is final; evaluation is left to right;
       no undefined behavior); a bug history of about 190 bugs in 17
       classes shows that each class is one decision made in several places
       (the constant evaluators, merged into 'fold' on 2026-10-21, had about
       40 bugs and none since); each step: tests of its bugs first, a
       mechanical move, then the behavior change, both suites, measure
       against the previous binary, pause for a commit
       4.1. one way to quote source in a message (class Q, about 14 bugs, 3
            broke the assembler): about 258 "'{}'" format sites, two helpers
            ('statement::quoted_source', 'program::quoted_as_written'); a
            character literal is quoted twice at about 11 of them (V-2, F-5)
            => design: a type 'source_text' with a 'std::formatter' that
               quotes once and escapes control bytes ('printable'); messages
               format tokens through it, never "'{}'" around token text;
               the formatter in 'compile_error.hpp' replaces both helpers
            => also G-5: a type parameter in a message prints the type it
               names
            => rejected: escaping in 'compile_error' only, a message would
               still quote by hand and double the quotes
            => done (2026-10-23): a function 'in_quotes' (string, token,
               statement) instead of a type, it reads like the other message
               helpers; named so that ADL does not find 'std::quoted'; the
               185 format calls rewritten by a script; control bytes were
               already escaped where messages and comments are printed;
               'qa/lint/check-messages.py' rejects quotes around a
               placeholder; 'quoted_as_written' and 'quoted_source' gone;
               G-5 fixed; tests 1595-1597
       4.2. one name classifier (class N, about 40 bugs): "what this name
            denotes here" is decided at about 27 sites outside 'toc'
            ('decouple_impl.hpp', 'parse_lookahead.hpp', 'stmt_call.hpp',
            'stmt_const.hpp', 'stmt_identifier.hpp', 'stmt_def_func.hpp',
            'stmt_def_type.hpp', the built-ins), each with its own subset of
            kinds, order and hiding
            => design: 'toc::classify(name) -> name_kind', a variant {value,
               constant, builtin type, user type, function, generic
               function, built-in function, literal}; it owns the hiding
               rules of 'scope_stack::lookup' (visible globals, a later
               constant, a value hiding a type); the parse sites ask the
               kind they need through 'expect_value', 'expect_callable',
               'expect_array', which give one message per wrong kind ("'int'
               is a type, not a value")
            => removes: G-3 (point fix done in 'decouple_impl.hpp', test
               1594), G-6, F-2, F-3, F-4, C-5
            => invariant: a name gives the same verdict in every position
               that asks the same kind
            => rejected: more predicates in 'parse_lookahead.hpp', the
               current structure
            => done (2026-10-23): 'classify_term(tc, tk, tz) -> term' in
               'parse_lookahead.hpp' (it needs the character after the

       4.3. array sizes through 'fold' (residual of class K, about 5 bugs):
            'stmt_const' is a second evaluator with its own unary ops and a
            literal or name only (R7 F-2, R9 F-2, R10 F-2, R12 F-4)
            => design: the size is parsed as an expression at the default
               type and valued by 'fold_checked'; names classified by 4.2;
               type field sizes use the same function
            => invariant: one evaluator for every compile-time value
            => regression (found 2026-10-23 by 'test-cli.sh', address
               range): on rv32i a size folds at 'i32', so 'dat big =
               i8[3000000000]' is rejected, the target accepts data up to
               the address range; fix: fold a size at 'i64', a size counts
               storage and is no value of the program, as 'stmt_const' did;
               then restore 0734 and 0829 and remove the '.rv32i.out' files
               of 0737-0739, 1322, 1323 and 1605
               => done (2026-10-23): the size is parsed at the default type
                  like an index and folds at 'i64'; 'array_length' folds at
                  any width at least its own (it folded at the default type
                  only, its comment said "a narrower width stays unfolded");
                  0734, 0829 restored, the '.rv32i.out' files gone except
                  1605's ('i32' is the default type on rv32i); no change of
                  code on any target
            => done (2026-10-23): 'stmt_const' replaced by 'stmt_array_size'
               in 'expr_any.hpp', an 'expr_any' at the default type parsed
               like an index and valued by 'constant_value'; the type field
               reads it too; a size is any constant expression ('i8[n * 2]',
               'i8[array_length(b) + 1]'); a name gives the verdict it gives
               as an index (F-4); on rv32i a size of 2^31 or more is a
               constant that does not fit 'i32', like an index (tests

       4.4. one written-part plan (class W, about 15 bugs, 4 silent wrong
            values): the order in which a literal writes its parts is
            decided by 'compile_field_list' and 'zero_remaining_elements'
            and again by 'list_written_parts', which misses the zero fill
            (V-1: 'q = {{1}, q.a[3]}' reads 0); a scalar in-place write is
            decided by predicates apart from the emit
            ('destination_is_clobbered_before_read' in 'expr_arith.hpp',
            the one in 'expr_bool.hpp', 'statement::reads_destination')
            => design: 'expr_type' builds once the list of parts in write
               order, each a byte range and its value or "zero"; the
               compile emits it, rule A checks it; 'expr_arith' and
               'expr_bool' build their step list (in place or through a
               scratch register) once, the compile emits it and the clobber
               check reads it
            => invariant: what is checked is what is emitted
            => rejected: adding the zero fill to 'list_written_parts', the
               order stays decided twice
            => steps (2026-10-23), the literal half first, the scalar half
               (step list of 'expr_arith', 'expr_bool') planned after it:
               4.4.1. tests of V-1, each reads 0 today: an array field
                      'q = {{1}, q.a[3]}', a record field 'o = {{1},
                      o.i.y}', a zero field 'o = {{}, o.i.y}', an array of
                      records 'ps = {{1}, {ps[0].y}}', an array of records
                      in a field 'q = {{{1}}, q.ps[1].x}'
               4.4.2. the plan: 'written_part' becomes a variant of
                      'value_write' {item, path}, 'bytes_write' {constant
                      bytes or a string, path} and 'zero_fill' {path of the
                      object, offset, size, alignment, comment label, the
                      paths it covers}; the path is the steps (field or
                      constant index) from the destination, applied by

       4.11. decide after measuring:
             4.11.1. fixed-register constraints before operands (class X,
                     about 7 bugs, open R7 B-1): an operation that needs
                     named registers declares its clobber set first and the
                     operands compile under an exclusion; changes register
                     choice, so measure and ask first
             4.11.2. noinline heights and recursion from the call graph
                     after the parse; may be covered by the fixed point of
                     4.10
             => measured (2026-10-23): the premise of 4.11.1 is the code
                today: an operation that needs named registers reserves them
                ('alloc_named_register', 'reserve_named_register') before it
                compiles its operands, and that is the cause of the class:
                an operand that needs one of them is rejected, "register rcx
                is always used by the operation at 8:5 ... compute the
                operand with 'let'", e.g. 'array_copy(a, b, count << shift)'
                (0727) and 'write(1, text, n / 2)' (1124) on x86_64; a named
                register that holds a scratch value is rejected under
                pressure, "cannot allocate register rdx because it holds a
                scratch value" (0535, 0726, rv32i 0729); 10 test outputs
                reject valid code this way, the verdict depends on the target
             => proposed refactor (asked 2026-10-23): operands first, the
                constraints at the operation: an operation compiles its
                operands into ordinary scratch registers (the named ones stay
                last resort), then at the instruction it saves the live
                values its clobber set holds, moves the operands into the
                named registers as one parallel move (a cycle through a free
                register or 'xchg'), emits and restores; the version that
                compiles an operand straight into its named register, as
                today, is kept where it is free and no other operand needs
                it, both measured by 'emit_most_efficient'; one helper per
                backend for the parallel move and the save, the built-ins
                and the division, shift and copy operations declare their
                register needs as data
             => removes: "always used by the operation" and "cannot allocate
                register ... scratch value"; stays: running out of scratch
                registers as a whole, and R7 B-1 (the address registers of
                noinline arguments held until the frame is written, its own
                refactor: write each slot when its address is computed)

[x] hard: constants: one system for values known at compile time (plan
    2026-10-21, waits for go-ahead; aliasing follows as its own plan)
    => problem: seven evaluators of a constant ('folded_constant' and its
       overrides, 'constant_value_of'/'constant_wrap', 'side_constant'/
       'typed_constant', 'element_constant'/'arith_constant', the convert
       'constant_value'/'narrow', 'make_constant_operand', the 'stmt_call'
       alias folds), each with its own answer to typedness, fit, where unary
       ops apply and overflow; the overflow check is toggled on 'toc'
       (pause and resume); verdicts differ by spelling: G6-1, G6-2, G6-3,
       G4-1, G5-1, ARCH-6, G5-2, 'array_length' typed in an assignment and
       untyped in a comparison
    => the system, one rule per function:
       1. a constant is a variable whose value is known: a typed constant
          (a conversion, a constant argument of an inlined call,
          'array_length') gets every verdict a variable of its type gets;
          an untyped one (a literal, a 'let') has no type and is checked by
          value where it is used
       2. a use is the width a value is computed at and whether its
          arithmetic is checked for overflow: 'value_use', built only by
          'toc::use_of(dst)' and 'toc::use_at(type)'
       3. one fit rule for a leaf: typed by type, untyped by value; every
          check (assignment, list element, comparison side, argument) calls
          it, only the messages differ
       4. one evaluator, the virtual 'fold(tc, use) ->
          std::optional<folded_value>': 'value' wrapped to the use width,
          'own_type' null when untyped; 'fold_checked' adds the fit check
          of rule 3 at the same width, so a folded value is rejected where a
          computed one is; 'fold' itself does not check, a conversion
          computes its argument at a narrower width on purpose; overrides:
          identifier, list, conversion, 'array_length', zero, 'expr_any',
          'expr_bool'
       5. unary ops apply by 'unary_ops' on a register at least as wide as
          the use; the widths of a conversion are one struct
          'conversion_widths{converted, argument, ops}' that its fold and
          its compile share
       6. the overflow policy is a field of 'destination' ('as_wrapping()'
          for a conversion argument); 'toc::checks()' never changes
       7. a value in a numeric position has a built-in non-record type,
          checked at parse ('assert_number')
       8. a constant reaches a destination only through the list that
          holds it ('expr_arith::compile_constant', the immediate by
          'folded_value::operand_at'); the nodes inside a list never emit
          a constant
       9. a constant is kept as a 'folded_value' wherever it is stored
          (aliases, 'ident_info', the frame constants), typedness is not
          derived again
       10. a 'let' of a typed initializer keeps the type, e.g. 'let v =
          int(5)' is checked like 'let v = i8(5)', by type
    => deleted: 'folded_constant', 'is_typed_constant' (4),
       'constant_value_of', 'constant_wrap', 'single_operand_type',
       'side_constant', 'typed_constant', 'make_constant_operand', the
       convert 'constant_value'/'narrow', the expr_bool
       'assert_constant_fits', 'overflow_check_pause'/'resume',
       'overflow_requested_'
    => rejected:
       1. a fix at each bug site: seven evaluators remain and drift again
       2. pause and resume narrowed to the call body: still state toggled at
          a distance that each new caller of a fold must know
       3. the overflow policy as a 'compile' parameter: about 40 overrides
          for 6 users; 'destination' already carries properties of the use
       4. a one-node IR evaluated or emitted: touches every emitter, while
          the rules diverge, not the evaluators
       5. a fold without the fit check, checked by callers: the contract
          that G5-1 broke
    => invariant: a typed constant and a variable of the same type and
       value give the same verdict and result; an overflow or zero divisor
       certain at compile time equals the run-time panic
    => steps, one diff each; R: byte-identical on the gate (all tests, the

    in quadratic time (20k terms 1.4 s, 300k terms over 120 s) and 50k type
    definitions take 7.9 s; find the quadratic step of each (review round 5,
    G7-11)
    => discarded (2026-10-21): the steps are quadratic only for inputs real
       programs do not reach, not worth a second code path
       1. the long sum: the column of a token is found by scanning its line
          back to the start, quadratic only in the length of one line of
          hundreds of thousands of characters
       2. the type and function tables, and the frames of variables (25k
          'var' in one function 10.5 s), are a 'lut' searched linearly per
          definition, measurable only from tens of thousands of definitions
       3. a line start table and a 'lut' with a hash index were built and
          reverted for that reason

    => problem: 'var' accepts two spellings of a value whose type the

[x] constant known at compile: fold constant elements in expressions
    ('expr_arith')
    => folded at compile, not parse: inline parameters become constant
       aliases per expansion and the width is the destination's
    => virtual 'statement::folded_constant(tc, width_type)', overridden by
       'expr_arith': recursive, unsigned arithmetic, truncated and sign
       extended to the width after each operation ('i8 = (100 + 100) / 2' is
       -28 like at run time); left to run time: divisor 0, MIN / -1, shift

       sub-expression is an immediate operand ('b / (1 + 1)')
    => lists of '+'/'-', only '*', '&', '|' or '^' merge their constants into
       one value applied last ('b + 3 + c * 2' -> 'b + 11'), or first when
       the first run-time element is subtracted ('1 - b + 2' -> '3 - b');
       an identity is not emitted ('b ^ 1 ^ 1'); nested lists fold

       above
[x] constant folding in lists mixing '*' with '/' or '%' ('expr_arith')
    => the constants of a run of '*' merge at its end past run-time factors
       ('2 * b * 3 / c' -> 'b * 6 / c')
    => consecutive constant divisors merge while their product fits the
       width ('b / 2 / 3' -> 'b / 6'); left to run time: divisors 0 and -1
       (MIN / -1 traps or wraps), a run-time divisor between constants
    => merging nothing keeps the list as written, a merged 1 changes
       nothing and is only a comment
[x] rv32i: a zero, copy or compare loop at a known offset from s0/s1 inside a
    word keeps one width throughout; peel 'sb'/'sh' up to the word boundary
    before the loop, e.g. 'var big[80] i8' at an odd offset loops 80 'sb'
    => 'plan_loop_start' picks the widest loop width every start reaches
       after the same head (a byte and/or a halfword); 'bulk_addresses_'
       keeps both starts; zero and known-size copy loops store the head
       unrolled from the direct offsets, 'equal' accesses it through the
       pointers and loads the count without it, 'array_copy' and
       'arrays_equal' check the run-time count before each head access and
       leave a shorter count to the existing tail; 'var big[80] i8' at
       's0 + 9' is 'sb', 'sh', a 19 'sw' loop and 'sb'; starts at different
       positions within a word (e.g. 1 and 3 B past a boundary) reach
       halfwords after a byte; x86 unchanged, rv32i output changes in 16
       tests (most only move the 'equal' count 'li' after the width comment);
       test 592 (every run-time count 0 to 9, noinline frame) with its loop
       widths in tests/0588.rv32i.widths, tests/0586.rv32i.widths 'd'/'e'
       now words
    => consolidated: class 'bulk_operation' in machine_rv32i.hpp decides the
       head, unrolled run or loop and tail ('walk_known_size',
       'walk_from_pointers', 'walk_runtime_count'); 'bulk_zero', 'bulk_copy'
       and 'bulk_compare' only implement 'do_operation'; a known size now
       has a tail decided at compile time and one chunk has no loop, so
       known-size copies and 'equal' shrank (e.g. test 398 257 -> 177
       instructions, none larger); test-bulk-widths.sh checks the width
       decision comment ('N-byte accesses[ after a H B head]'), which also
       covers zero loops and 'equal' without a loop
    => replaced by plain functions (same output): 'zero_known_size',
       'copy_known_size', 'compare_known_size', 'copy_runtime_count' and
       'compare_runtime_count' are written out one after the other for human
       readability, the repeated code is on purpose; only the plain decisions
       are shared ('aligned_parts', 'plan_loop_start', 'plan_known_loop')
[x] rv32i: a byte aligned zero, copy or compare at a known offset from s0/s1
    uses byte accesses throughout; peel 'sb'/'sh' up to the word boundary,
    then 'sw' and the tail, e.g. 20 bytes at 's0 + 329' is 8 stores instead
    of a 20 iteration 'sb' loop (etc/codegen-inefficiencies.baz)
    => unrolled zero (up to 16 stores), copy (up to 16 bytes) and constant
       stores take at each offset the widest access that fits and is aligned
       for every address ('for_each_aligned_part', 'start_of'); 20 bytes at
       's0 + 329' is 'sb', 'sh', 4 'sw', 'sb'; a copy needs both addresses at
       the same position within a word to widen; peeled stores count for the
       zero unroll cap; an assembly comment names the parts and the start
       when peeling widened them, and a zero loop names the start it did not
       peel; loops are the open medium item; x86 unchanged; test 591,
       tests/0553.rv32i.stores updated (7 bytes: immediates instead of 'la'
       and 4 load/store pairs)
[x] rv32i: 'array_copy', 'equal' and 'arrays_equal' use only the type alignment;
    derive a larger alignment from the addresses as 'copy' and 'zero' do
    => 'set_array_copy_source/destination' (also used by
       'set_memory_equal_left/right') keep the smallest 'access_alignment' of

[-] a 'read' into a 'bool' array stores any byte, but copying a 'bool'
    ('is_bool_copy') relies on 0 or 1; reject 'bool' arrays in 'read'
    => kept: the goal is an efficient conversion of any type to and from
       bytes, so 'read' stays a raw byte copy. truth tests already treat
       non-zero as true, but '==', 'equal', 'arrays_equal', bool to integer
       copies and 'write' use the byte as is, so a stored 'bool' stays 0 or 1.
       bytes from 'read' can be normalized with an additional pass over the
       'bool' array
[-] lut: change to map
    => keep it simple for now
    => the frame tables 'consts_', 'vars_' and 'aliases_' hold a few entries
       and are created and destroyed per block, loop and inlined call; a
       linear scan over a small contiguous vector beats a map at that size
       and most keys fit the small string buffer
    => a map allocates a node per entry ('unordered_map' also buckets and
       hashing) on every frame push, likely outweighing the lookup savings
    => only the global 'funcs_' and 'types_' grow with the program, still
       tens of entries
    => 'put' accepts duplicate keys and 'get_const_ref' returns the first
       match; a map would change that behavior
[-] some improvements (performance wise) of toc::indent using a buffer on the
    stack
    => do perf tests first using etc/perf.sh
    => 'toc::indent' no longer exists; the assemblers' 'indentation' builds a
       short string that is concatenated into the owned line text right away,
       fits the small string buffer up to indent 3 and does not show in the
       compile-only perf reports (malloc in total about 1.5%); a stack buffer
       would still need the owned line string

[x] a more detailed compilation option: bounds check with line numbers
    cmp r14, 8
    mov rsi, 42           ; Line number for first check
    cmovge rax, rsi       ; Move line number to rax if r14 >= 8
    jge panic_bounds

    ; For the second check
    cmp rcx, 8
    mov rsi, 87           ; Line number for second check
    cmovg rax, rsi        ; Move line number to rax if rcx > 8
    jg panic_bounds
    => ./baz prog.baz 65536 checked line

[x] an efficient way of comparing two arrays or instances:
    bool equal = array_equal(src, dst, nelements)
      works with instances also when nelements is 1 
    ; Compare two memory regions
    ; rsi = pointer to slice1
    ; rdi = pointer to slice2
    ; rcx = length in bytes
    ; Returns: ZF=1 if equal, ZF=0 if different
    cld              ; Clear direction flag (forward)
    repe cmpsb       ; Repeat while equal, compare bytes
    ; After: ZF=1 if equal, ZF=0 if not equal
    je equal
    jne not_equal
    => arrays_equal(arr1, arr2, nelements)
